Round 19b has not been re-scored. After four walkthrough runs on a plateau (5.48 · 5.50 · 5.60 · ≈5.4), Opus's call is that **further static frames will not close the gate; the next evidence is a clickable first run with real drivers and the mounted glance test** (§10, §11 R14, R16).


**Round 19b (2026-10-27).** From Opus `272` and Codex `271`: D1's two newest rows carry their own rates (fixture `today_afternoon_trips`: 16:04 $11.04/km, 17:21 $11.40/km; no "$…/km"); G4d draws the Semáforo screen paused (B5's door); G4c says what to do ("No tienes que hacer nada; pasa cuando la navegación llena la pantalla") and the cause has one name, "no cupo en la pantalla"; A3b's Kompara row uses the unboxed verde K (Codex's hard gate: no K in a square); A1 defines "neto" once ("neto: ya restamos gasolina y desgaste"); basis lines drop "según el lector" (the source lives in the Cómo se calculó sheets); E1c draws the day without trips as a short bar below the $0 line (−$214); **one cause for the outage** in the overlay tab, the notification and Hoy — "Falta el permiso de Accesibilidad" (C6: "Semáforo apagado / Falta el permiso · abre Kompara al estacionarte"; H6: "Se apagó el semáforo"; B2's hero); **G2c** edits tu mínimo ($6.50) next to G2's tu meta edit, so the walkthrough's task 6 has its own frame. **Evidence tooling:** `design-system/ui_kits/android-app-v2/prototype.html` (generated by `make-prototype.py --check`: every screen at real size, 122 wired tap links, the eight walkthrough tasks with a facilitator log) and `docs/ux-elevation/driver-test-plan.md` (the real-driver walkthrough and the mounted glance test that settle "comparable in use").

### 9p Pre-test — one door to Semáforo, one moment per task, one outage (both Round 19b reviews)

**Why.** Round 19b's confirmation passed both reviewers on both rubrics — Codex 71/80 + 34/40 ([`281`](ux-elevation/debate/281-round19b-codex-astra.md): A3b's hard gate cleared, E1c and D1 resolved) and Opus 75/80 + 34/40 ([`282`](ux-elevation/debate/282-round19b-opus-5-5.md): navigation 10 → 9 for B1's door, now a measured repeat; no hard fail) — and both kept "comparable in use" at NOT_YET with one next step, the real-driver test in [`driver-test-plan.md`](ux-elevation/driver-test-plan.md), once the prototype stops contaminating it. Their lists name what a driver would hit: Hoy at 14:40 drilled into the 21:10 day, so one tap turned $548 into $1,026 — a money inconsistency the plan's gate fails on, caused by the prototype, not the design; B3 and B4 opened "Semáforo funcionando · Lectura comprobada · hace 2 min" before any offer was read; every offer row opened the 14:32 verde detail; the limit rings jumped from $6.00 to $6.50 and Guardar returned to $6.00; and, on the frames, B1 as the only Hoy door that did not open Semáforo, C6's cause crammed into the 56dp plate, B2's "desde las 7:50" against H6's 20:31 notice, and G4c saying its cause twice.

**The driver test plan** ([`driver-test-plan.md`](ux-elevation/driver-test-plan.md)) takes Opus's conditions (`282` §4): an Uber baseline — each driver rates three matched tasks in their own Uber Driver app (today's earnings, last week's, one trip's detail) on the same Single Ease Question, and "comparable" means Kompara's mean ease on those tasks within 0.5 of Uber's, the 1–10 rating kept as secondary; task 8 on a real Android Settings hand-off, the one step the prototype's A3b → B3 skips; the first tap of tasks 6 and 7 logged (the prototype's facilitator log keeps it, with each attempt's limit events and saved pair); and a decision pre-registered for ten drivers (a 95 % interval of about ±0.6 on mean ease): ≥ 6.0 passes, 5.6–5.99 adds five drivers, below 5.6 fails, and a single task under 5.5 is a flag unless it is also a top-named problem. The mounted glance test runs apart, with "¿La tomarías?" after each yellow trial (§11 R14).

**Not applied (open):** C2's amarillo as a decision (four of six judges in runs 3 and 4; the mounted test's "¿La tomarías?" probe measures whether drivers want one, §11 R14); G4c's row still opens the fixture D1, whose 12:10 row reads "Abajo de tu mínimo" (Opus R19-M7, Codex R19b; accepted at caption level as an alternate destination — drawn in the pre-test fixes as D1b, below); D1 does not scroll in the prototype (Opus R19b-P4: reaching D1 counts as success on task 3); A3 and A3b's two "Abrir ajustes"; **E1d's first row still reads "según el lector, antes de gasolina, desgaste y coche"** (§5.5), which the checker's new stale pattern rejects — Opus R19b-R2's choice is still open (give D3b E1d's sub-line, or drop the source from both; the pre-test fixes dropped it from both, below); the mounted glance test and a timed install. Superseded here: §9n row 1's rule for the healthy door ("the door opens what its line names": the limits at rest while nothing waits) and every note that puts the limits one tap from Hoy; §9o's open items "the healthy door's destination" and "G4's preparado state … not drawn"; G4c's "No tienes que hacer nada; pasa cuando la navegación llena la pantalla" and "La oferta quedó en Tu día"; C6's 240 × 56 plate and the notification's "Le falta un permiso"; B2's "desde las 7:50"; Hoy at 14:40 opening the 21:10 Tu día and Cómo se calculó; every offer row opening the 14:32 detail; the prototype's fixed limits.

Not changed: la regla's geometry and states and every chip frame (C1–C5, C7–C12); the money chain ($548, $1,026, $4,980, $12,169); the fixture's raw inputs — D1a's and D3a's figures derive from shift 1 and the 14:32 offer ($831 + $148 = $979 over 64.3 + 12.0 = 76.3 km), D2b's and D2c's from C3's and C2's offers. The night set is regenerated from its twins: N1's door and N2's "desde ayer 20:31" follow B1 and B2, and N3–N10 are unchanged. `check-fixture.py` checks 166 figures in 58 frames, 18 chips and 60 offer rows and 23 spec strings; at the time of writing it reports one problem, E1d's "según el lector," (open, above), and no spec failure. ### 9q Round 20 — the answers hidden, one minute for the month, one decision rule (both Round 20 reviews)

**Why.** Round 20 confirmed the pre-test kit and its fixes. Both reviewers passed both rubrics — **Codex 71/80 + 34/40** ([`291`](ux-elevation/debate/291-round20-codex-astra.md): scores unchanged; the Round 19b items resolved, the 14:40 snapshot only on the Tu día branch) and **Opus 76/80 + 34/40** ([`292`](ux-elevation/debate/292-round20-opus-5-5.md): navigation 9 → 10, the measured repeat gone; no hard fail) — and both found the kit and the prototype **NOT READY as written** for the same three blockers. **The answers were visible:** the facilitator panel beside the phone printed every task's "Éxito" path, task 7's route included, and on a phone it sat under the fold, so the facilitator had to scroll the participant's screen to press Hecho. **The month was the 21:10 day under a 14:40 clock:** B1 → Ganancias → Mes → Por semana read "19 oct · hoy · 9 viajes $1,026" and $12,169 beside Hoy's and E8's $548 — three taps from task 4's start, and the plan fails on a reported money inconsistency. **The plan's decision rules disagreed:** the walkthrough gate's "ease against Uber ≥ 7.5" and "no task below 5.5", the baseline paragraph's secondary rating and the pre-registered flag rule, with no Kompara task to match Uber's trip detail. Both kept "comparable in use" at NOT_YET: the repairs improve the instrument and supply no human evidence. Opus added seven measures to fix in the same pass (N4–N10) and three kit nits; Codex two (E8 → G5's dismissal, the glance scoring).

**Applied in this version** (frames, captions, the fixture, the checker, the prototype and the plan; `check-fixture.py` asserts the 14:40 month in E3, E3d, D3b, E4 and E5, G4h's and G1b's words and D1a's and D1b's ten rows, bans "21:10" on E3, E3d and D3b, "Redondeado" on E3d and D3b and "Ofertas · 23" on D1, and treats the old month figures as stale):

| # | Finding | Decision | Frames |
|---|---|---|---|

**Round 20b — the confirmation** ([`294`](ux-elevation/debate/294-round20b-codex-astra.md), [`295`](ux-elevation/debate/295-round20b-opus-5-5.md)). **Codex** (`294`): every one of its Round 20 findings resolved; scores unchanged, **71/80 + 34/40**, PASS / PASS, no hard fail; **no-go only for three plan items** — task 8 run first, before any exposure to A3 (task 7 ends on A3 and would rehearse the first-day setup); the comprehension gates judged over the final cohort if the sample grows to fifteen drivers (8 of 10 or 12 of 15, and no negative-number misreading in the whole cohort); and the hand-off on the real Kompara build, no stand-in app. All three are applied in the plan. **Opus** (`295`, driven with real clicks, drags and touches in headless Chrome at two laptop and two phone sizes): all of N1–N10 and the kit nits resolved — every state a task reaches in tasks 1, 3, 4 and 9 reads 14:40 · 82 %, every ledger foots; scores unchanged, **76/80 + 34/40**, no hard fail; **go on a laptop as written**, but phone runs needed the back/reload fix: participant mode added no history entries, so Android's back gesture or a reload left the prototype and came back to D2 over its own 21:10 day ("$1,026 · 9 viajes tomados en 8 h 10 min" under a 14:40 clock) with "← Atrás" disabled — applied, with the rest of its list, in Round 20c. Comparable in use: Codex NOT_YET (no new human evidence); Opus feel yes, use at least as short as Uber on paper and still unmeasured.

### 9r Round 21 — the save that confirms, one permission screen, the meaning where the number is (walkthrough run 5 + both Round 21 proposals)

**Why.** Run 5 was the first walkthrough on the clickable prototype, not on contact sheets: six persona judges (Opus ×2, Sonnet ×3, Haiku ×1) drove `prototype.html` in participant mode through a harness that taps by visible text or coordinates, in the plan's task order (8, then 1–7, 9), and the prototype logged every tap ([`results-r20c-run5.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r20c-run5.md)). Every judge reached every answer screen (54 of 54 by the log; 53 yes and 1 partial by self-report), and mean ease rose to **5.83/7**, the first step off the plateau of runs 1–4 (5.48 · 5.50 · 5.60 · ≈5.4), with ease against Uber **7.0/10** and "would understand every screen" **7.0/10**. The walkthrough gate was still **not met**: the two tasks where the driver acts — changing tu mínimo and the first day — both scored **5.00**, and both left the drivers unsure whether their action had worked. Both proposals put those two first ([`301`](ux-elevation/debate/301-round21-codex-astra.md) Codex Astra, [`302`](ux-elevation/debate/302-round21-opus-5-5.md) Opus 5.5); both also listed money words the judges misread, and Opus added task 7's unanswered "why".

| Task (run order) | Self-reported success (yes / partial / no) | Reached the answer screen (log) | Mean taps | Mean misses | Mean time (s) | Mean ease (1–7) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 · First day setup | 5 / 1 / 0 | 6/6 | 4.7 | 0.2 | 34 | **5.00** |
| 1 · Hoy: kept today + is it reading | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 0.2 | 0.0 | 10 | 6.17 |
| 2 · Offer: worth it? | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 9 | 5.67 |
| 3 · Every offer today | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 1.0 | 0.0 | 16 | 6.00 |
| 4 · Last week after the car | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 2.3 | 0.0 | 22 | 5.83 |
| 5 · Which app paid more | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 1.0 | 0.0 | 12 | 6.50 |
| 6 · Change tu mínimo | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 9.7 | 0.0 | 51 | **5.00** |
| 7 · The semáforo stopped | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 2.7 | 0.0 | 27 | 6.17 |
| 9 · Last trip's detail | 6 / 0 / 0 | 6/6 | 1.0 | 0.0 | 14 | 6.17 |
| **Run 5** | 53 / 1 / 0 | 54/54 | | | | **5.83** · against Uber 7.0/10 · understand 7.0/10 |

The matched tasks against the judges' own estimate of the same task in Uber Driver: task 1 Kompara 6.17 vs Uber 5.83 (+0.33), task 4 5.83 vs 4.67 (+1.17), task 9 6.17 vs 4.67 (+1.50). **Gate** (the walkthrough's, §11 R16: mean ≥ 6.0, ease against Uber ≥ 7.5, no task under 5.5): **not met.** The judges are models, the Android step was imagined (the prototype answered «Abrir ajustes» with "no está en esta prueba") and the Uber scores are self-estimated, so run 5 directs the design and replaces no real driver.

**Decided in this version** (frames, captions, the prototype, the checker, the plan; the kit's Round 21 CSS blocks only add classes — the worded period steps and the limits' filled field):

|---|---|---|---|

Kompara lo usa para leer tus ofertas; nunca toca botones ni acepta viajes por ti.", "Semáforo apagado", **"Abrir ajustes"** · "Ahora no"; it fits without scrolling. **A2 gives the risk its own line** (Opus `302` §2; Codex `301` §2 also keeps it a separate sentence before consent): Lee loses the risk clause, and a fourth line in the same grammar, before "Acepto", reads **Uber y DiDi** "no son parte de Kompara y podrían no permitir apps como esta. ### 9s Round 22 — the reconciliation behind its own row, the per-app answer on Ganancias, the steps over Android's settings (walkthrough run 6)

**Why.** Run 6 drove the Round 21b prototype with the same six persona judges (Opus ×2, Sonnet ×3, Haiku ×1), the same harness (now printing the snackbar and leaving out covered text) and the same brief; the first-day note pointed to the Android stand-in, A3s ([`results-r21b-run6.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r21b-run6.md)). Mean ease rose to **5.93/7** (run 5: 5.83), "would understand every screen" to **7.2/10** (7.0), ease against Uber held at **7.0/10**. The two tasks Round 21 rebuilt both rose: **changing tu mínimo 5.00 → 6.00** (9.7 → 4.0 taps, 51 → 29 s) and **the first day 5.00 → 5.50**, now the lowest task. The walkthrough gate was still **not met**: the mean is under 6.0 and ease against Uber under 7.5, though no task is under 5.5. One finding was unanimous: **all six judges stumbled on E1's reconciliation row**, "Uber reporta $34 más de lo que leímos" over "Leímos $5,028 · tu resumen dice $5,062" — three figures under the $4,980 that matched nothing else on the screen (Uber only and before costs, against the week's $8,400 for both apps), and j6's tap on its chevron reached the "no está en esta prueba" note. **Task 5 fell from 6.50 to 5.67** (1.0 → 3.2 taps) on a detour: j2, j5 and j6 looked for the Uber / DiDi split on Ganancias, opened the week's ledger (E1d), went back and only then found Comparar.

The judges are models, the Android step is still imagined and the Uber scores are self-estimated, so run 6 directs the design and replaces no real driver.

**Also aligned** in the integration pass: the prototype wires E1's "Cuadrar con tu resumen de Uber" → E1e (its scrim, handle and back return to E1 through the history, so closing it leaves no entry behind) and "Por app" → F1, drops the old row's link, gives E1e's "Traer otro resumen" the note (the import flow is not wired in this test, as E6's "Traer un resumen"), and its facilitator answers name "te deja por km" (task 3), E1e as the reconciliation rather than the answer (task 4) and E1's Por app route (task 5); `check-fixture.py` asserts E1's rows, E1d's split, E1e's basis and figures, "te deja por km" on D1a, D1b and D1, drops E1's "$34 más", treats "Uber reporta $34 más", "Leímos $5,028" and "neto por km" as stale in frames and carries "Cuadrar con tu resumen de Uber" in this spec; the night set was regenerated (N3's header, N4's three rows) and N3's and N4's captions re-written; `sheets.py` and the kit README list E1e (111 frames). `make-prototype.py --check`: 286 link specs, 286 matched, 0 unmatched, 0 conflicts, 440 hotspots, nine tasks (108 screens, with G2d, G2e and G4i drawn live). `check-fixture.py` checks 272 figures in 81 frames, 20 chips and 86 offer rows and 27 spec strings, and passes.

**Still open (after Round 22):** E1e covers the row that opened it (a sheet under that row would need 370dp where 351dp is free), so its title repeats what was tapped; Comparar still draws a DiDi rate for a driver who drove only Uber (j1: "Solo manejaste Uber esta semana" and Tu ciudad instead), not drawn; Hoy's limits line does not read as a door to the editor (j2), unchanged; the amarillo chip's verdict word (j4, j5) stays the founder's R14 decision for the mounted glance test (§11 R14); the trip's "te deja" before the car beside the day's "te quedaron" after it still reads as two meanings to j2, j3 and j6 (a per-trip car share was proposed by j6); D2–D2d's sub-line still says "neto · $285/h · 24 min", the word run 6 found unclear in the list's header; "te deja por km" keeps its 13sp grey size against three judges' "small"; the toast's dark look is the kit's — on Android 12+ text toasts follow the system theme — and in the kit it stays on screen so the walkthrough sees it; in the prototype the neutral note sits 12dp over A3's footer for 2.2 s and covers the "Semáforo apagado" status line while it shows; E1b, E1c and E1d's scrims still push E1 onto the history (their back then reopens the sheet), where E1e's go back; "Por app" doubles the Comparar tab's door, admitted because it carries its amount. Engineering: whether Android opens Kompara's own accessibility page per version and maker (row 6) and the battery cause in B2 (§9r row 13). Round 22 has not been re-scored on either rubric; the next evidence is walkthrough run 7 on this prototype, then the real-driver test and the mounted glance test (§10, §11 R14, R16).

### 9t Round 23 — a yellow true at the minimum, the day beside the trip, the recovery drawn, the instrument corrected (walkthrough runs 5–7 + both Round 23 proposals)

**Why.** Run 7 drove the Round 22 prototype with the same six persona judges (Opus ×2, Sonnet ×3, Haiku ×1), the same harness and the same brief, which added one question, money figures that contradict ([`results-r22-run7.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r22-run7.md)). Mean ease held at **5.94/7** (run 6: 5.93), ease against Uber rose to **7.3/10** (7.0) and "would understand every screen" to **7.3/10** (7.2); the lowest tasks sat at 5.67 (the first day, the offer, changing tu mínimo, the stopped semáforo), so no task is under 5.5, but the mean and the score against Uber miss the gate again. Two readings shaped the round. **A repeated defect, not the mean, blocks the gate** (Opus [`312`](ux-elevation/debate/312-round23-opus-5-5.md)): §11 R16 also fails a version on a defect named by ≥ 4 of 6 judges that the previous run named, and the trip's money against the day's was in the top three for four judges in run 6 (j2, j3, j4, j6) and again in run 7 (j1, j2, j3, j5, with j4 and j6 close). **The ledger had been over-read** (Codex [`311`](ux-elevation/debate/311-round23-codex-astra.md) §5): run 7 reached 52 of 54 answer screens, task 7 at 4/6, not 54/54 objective successes; its one money report (the tally's rule 5, which failed) named both the undated "Ayer te quedaron $1,026 aprox.", which j6 checked against Sunday 18 oct's bar, and the car-basis distinction; and text-assisted tapping cannot establish legibility or target accuracy, nor imagined Android and self-estimated Uber tasks comparative use. Runs 5–7 on the clickable prototype ([`results-r20c-run5.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r20c-run5.md), [`results-r21b-run6.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r21b-run6.md), run 7):

Self-reported success: run 5, 53 yes and 1 partial; run 6, 53 and 1; run 7, 54 and 0. **Gate** (§11 R16: mean ≥ 6.0, ease against Uber ≥ 7.5, no task under 5.5): **not met** in any of the three. The judges are models, their Uber scores estimates from two screenshots, and their scores are compressed: in run 7 only 6 of 54 ratings were 7, five of them on task 1, which needs no tap, because the brief asked for hesitations on every task (Opus `312` §5) — a mean of 6.0 is a ceiling of how these judges rate, not a design threshold.

**Decided in this version** (frames, captions, the prototype, the checker, the plan; no new CSS — `base-v3.css` only corrects its chip comment). Where the two proposals differ, the founder's brief settled it:

**The instrument, corrected before run 8** (both proposals, §5): the walkthrough's own scenario produced some of its findings (the repeated distrust of the DiDi figures came from personas who drive Uber only). **Personas match the fixture, traits kept:** all six drive Uber and DiDi, mostly Uber (the fixture has 34 Uber trips and 28 DiDi), and all six already entered Lo del coche at $1,500 a week (la cuenta for renters, crédito y seguro for owners); Rosa and Ana lose "Uber only" and Jorge "DiDi mostly" (which also clashed with «Abrir Uber para probar»); age, comfort with apps, colour blindness, large text and impatience stay; an Uber-only fixture remains separate coverage and never excuses a missing first-day cost question (Codex). **One framing sentence after task 8**, the same in the real-driver plan: «Ahora imagina que ya llevas semanas usando Kompara. En esta prueba manejas Uber y DiDi, más Uber, y ya le pusiste lo que pagas del coche: $1,500 a la semana.» **No invented errors:** the brief's "hesitate and misread" becomes «Describe dificultades observadas; no inventes errores para representar al personaje.» and «Describe hasta tres dificultades; si no encontraste ninguna, dilo.» (Codex). **Task 2 is screenshot-only for a model:** the harness prints no screen text in task 2 (printed text made a glance a reading task), and the prompt no longer promises an accept/skip answer — «Te llega una oferta en Uber. Mírala una vez, de reojo: ¿qué te dice esta oferta? Luego: ¿La tomarías? ¿Por qué?», whose second question has no right answer; its score still never picks the phrase, R14 does. **Task 3** asks «Desde Hoy, encuentra todas las ofertas que te llegaron hoy, con lo que te deja cada una por km.», the column's own words. **Task 7** matches its world (the screen says 8:01 and "desde ayer 20:31"; j2 noticed that "durante tu turno" did not): «Empiezas tu turno temprano y el semáforo no sale en tus ofertas. Averigua por qué y qué hacer.» Run 8 is therefore **not strictly comparable** with runs 5–7; the changed instrument and the endpoint are declared before it runs, and a higher mean is not read as an isolated design gain (Codex `311` §6).

**The stop rule (both reviewers): one run 8, then no more model score-chasing.** Run 8 is a bounded readiness pass on the corrected instrument — the new copy, the day row, the recovery's two branches, E1f, the limits path — and there is no run 9, whatever its mean. **Opus retires the `252` gate (mean ≥ 6.0, against Uber ≥ 7.5) for model runs:** the design is ready for real drivers when run 8 shows all six — (1) 54/54 answer screens reached, with misses ≤ 0.2 per task; (2) no task under 5.5; (3) no defect in the top three of 4 or more judges in both run 7 and run 8; (4) no money contradiction that is actually true of the fixture; (5) Kompara at or above the judges' own Uber estimate on tasks 1, 4 and 9; (6) B1's $548 read as after the car by 6 of 6. The mean and the score against Uber are reported, not gated. Codex accepts **"estructuralmente comparable; listo para probar con conductores"** once both rubrics still pass and the revised branches and money states are coherent. **"Comparable in use to Uber Driver" is never claimed from model judges:** only from real drivers under the plan's pre-registered rule — all five gates, among them a mean of at least 6.0 over the nine tasks and Kompara within 0.5 of the same drivers' own Uber ease on tasks 1, 4 and 9, with the actual Android return — plus the mounted glance test for the chip (R14: ≥ 95 % correct, median response ≤ 1 s, no yellow read as a loss). The plan's "add five" and its Uber comparison do not apply to six model personas.

**Also aligned** in the integration pass: the prototype (`make-prototype.py`) — the live regla's yellow phrase and spoken label; task 7's 20 oct routes (A3s with its toast, «Ya lo activé» → B2r replacing B2 in the history so back leaves Kompara, «Regresar sin activarlo» → A3n, B2r's "Que no se vuelva a apagar" → A4b and back, B2r's door, primary, ⚙ and tabs giving the neutral note); the details' day row taking its figure and door from the opener ($548 → D3a over B1, B7, B9, D1a and D1b; $1,026 → D3 over D1; B5's $434 → the note), each sheet opening on a line that cuts no text behind it (when " a esa hora" wraps, the gap above the actions shrinks first, to no less than 7dp, then the sheet opens one line higher) and refitting once the fonts load; "Por app" → E1f; tasks 2, 3 and 7 in the new wording, with new answers for tasks 2, 5, 7 and 9. `check-fixture.py` asserts the day row on D2, D2c's "cumple tu mínimo de $6.00/km", the dated lines on B2, B2r and B4, B2r and E1f, A4's steps and "Que no se vuelva a apagar" on B2 and B2r, and treats "Entre tu meta y tu mínimo", "Que no vuelva a pasar" and "Ayer te quedaron" as stale in frames. The night set was regenerated and N2's, N3's, N4's and N9's captions re-written (`make-part-n.py`); `_head.html`'s lede and `base-v3.css`'s chip comment give the new phrase (and 312 = 240 × 1.3); `sheets.py`, the kit README and the appendix list B2r and E1f (113 frames); the driver-test plan carries tasks 2, 3 and 7, the framing sentence, the D2 probe (R17, now part of the plan's comprehension gate) and R14's yellow answer, "cumple" or "entre". `make-prototype.py --check`: 304 link specs, 304 matched, 0 unmatched, 0 conflicts, 458 hotspots, nine tasks (110 screens, with G2d, G2e and G4i drawn live). `check-fixture.py` checks 288 figures in 84 frames, 20 chips and 86 offer rows and 27 spec strings, and passes.

**Still open (after Round 23):** B2r's "Que no se vuelva a apagar" opens A4b, whose status still reads "Semáforo apagado · aún falta el permiso" with the permission back (A4 does not fit either: "La primera oferta…" is the first run's), so a battery step in B2r's world, or a status line that follows the state, is undrawn; B2r's door opens G4e in the kit, whose "Tu primera oferta lo confirma" and 9:44 are the first run's, so the prototype gives the note there — a 20 oct Semáforo preparado (with no "ayer") is undrawn; A3's 800 ms "✓ Permiso listo" before B3 or B2r is in the captions, not in the prototype; after the recovery, C13's "Abrir Kompara" and H6b's notice still open B2, reachable only by going back from B2r; over Hoy the prototype opens D2 at 208, so "Llevas hoy · aprox." stays above the sheet with its figure under it; B5 opens D2b with $434 and no breakdown drawn for that figure (the note), and D2d over B9 with changed limits leaves a 61dp gap (reachable only through `?screen=`); "Por app" keeps the Comparar glyph although it now stays on Ganancias, its sub-line stays 13sp, and E1f's 31dp from the finding to the column header is the loosest gap on the sheet; the neutral note on a sheet without a footer sits 120dp from the bottom and covers E1f's Uber row for 2.2 s; B4's dated line would reach about 361dp at a 115 % font, over the 353dp measure, and could leave "aprox." alone (a no-break "$284 aprox." would hold it); G2's $9.49 example sits just under the edited meta of $9.50, easy to confuse at 13sp (unchanged); G8's colours answer is still not drawn (its copy in §5.19 now uses the three phrases); `_regla-markup.html`'s C10 comment gives the card at 473 (top 311) where the markup and C10 use 469 (307.4); the model harness (TASKS.md, the personas, the judge prompt, task 2's text-free view) lives outside the repo and must take the corrections above before run 8. Carried: the verdict word stays the founder's R14 decision; Comparar's single-app state is still undrawn; the trip's figure after the car goes to the founder only if R17's renters still ask for it (3 of 5); whether Android opens Kompara's own accessibility and app pages per version and maker, and the battery cause in B2, are engineering's. Round 23 has not been re-scored on either rubric; the next evidence is run 8, then the real-driver test and the mounted glance test (§10, §11 R14, R16, R17).

### 9u Round 24 — the basis beside the number, one name for the row that counts (walkthrough run 8)

**Why.** Run 8 drove the Round 23b prototype on the corrected instrument (personas who drive Uber and DiDi, mostly Uber, with Lo del coche already entered at $1,500 a week; the framing sentence after task 8; no invented difficulty; task 2 screenshot-only), so it is not strictly comparable with runs 5–7 ([`results-r23b-run8.md`](ux-elevation/blind-test/walkthrough/results-r23b-run8.md)). It **met the walkthrough gate** for the first time: mean ease **6.17/7**, ease against Uber **7.5/10**, understand every screen 7.5/10, lowest task **5.67** (the first day and the stopped semáforo); **54/54** answer screens reached, 0.0 misses per task, 54 self-reported successes; Kompara above the judges' own Uber estimate by **+1.67 / +2.00 / +1.50** on tasks 1, 4 and 9. On **Opus's readiness criteria** (`312` §6, §9t) it meets **five of six**: (1) 54/54 with no misses; (2) no task under 5.5; (4) no money contradiction true of the fixture — the one report, $548 at 14:40 against the $1,026 the day ended at, is consistent (the harness's automatic rule 5 flagged it); (5) above the Uber estimates; (6) B1's $548 read as after the car by six of six. It misses (3): **the trip's money against the day's** was in the top three of four judges in run 7 (j1, j2, j3, j5) and again in run 8 (j2, j3, j5, j6), now about where the basis sits, not the definition. "$114" carried "antes de lo del coche" only in the small grey label above it (j2: easy to miss; j5 doubted it was the real number), and E1f's per-app rates carried theirs only in a grey note under them while the $4,980 behind the sheet includes the car (j3 read it twice; j6 called it a grey footnote). j3 asked for one fixed label per basis in the same place on every screen. A second finding was not repeated but was named by three judges (j2, j4, j5): Semáforo's "Ofertas sin semáforo · ninguna hoy" read as contradicting Hoy's unread 11:48 offer, which does get a grey semáforo.

**Decided in this version** (frames, captions, the prototype, the checker, the plan; no new CSS, the suffixes are inline styles in the frames):

D2's label becomes "Te dejó · aprox." over `$114 sin el coche` (D2c `$86`; D2b and D2d "Te habría dejado · aprox." over `$27`; the prototype's under-way "Te deja · aprox."). The words are money 500 `--ink-2` at 20sp on the figure's baseline (93dp), as the chip's "/km neto" follows its rate. El coche ($214) se resta por día.", two sentences where one figure and its basis fit; and a "con coche" tag on every total (Round 23's rejection stands, since the totals' quiet line already names the car) | D2, D2b, D2c, D2d |

## 10. Scorecard

Spec-level expert scores against [`debate/02-rubric-uber-bar.md`](ux-elevation/debate/02-rubric-uber-bar.md) (pass = every criterion ≥ 8 and total ≥ 68). Round 3 scored the first v2 draft at 59 (Opus) / 51 (Codex); Round 4 at 63 / 56; Round 5 at 66 / 58; **Round 6: Opus 68 — PASS at the threshold** (every criterion ≥ 8, no critical or high defects) and **Codex 63 — NOT_YET** (numbers 6, consistency 7); both lists are addressed in §9e. **Round 7 (confirmation): Opus 68 — PASS** (0 critical, 0 high, 1 medium, 9 low; all applied in §9e row 9; its verdict: build the chip and the readiness model first, on device, through the 1-second mounted test before any other screen); **Codex Round 7: 65 — NOT_YET** (numbers 7, consistency 7; its 2 high + 6 medium + 14 low are applied in §9e row 10; its verdict: build the capture → readiness → chip → dated-ledger slice first, including cancellation/undo and statement reconciliation, and test it on mounted phones). The six-lens judge panel (round 3, run on the Round 6 version) scored 55–63 per lens before its refutation stage died on the model limit; its high-severity findings were folded in by hand (§9e row 11) and the declined ones are recorded in §9f. **Round 8 (joint confirmation): Opus 68 — PASS** (0 critical, 0 high, 5 medium, 9 low) and **Codex 65 — NOT_YET** (numbers 7, consistency 7; 3 high). Both lists are applied in §9e row 12 — including the shared finding that the fixture checker tested presence anywhere on the page, now rewritten to assert each figure inside its named frame — **Round 9: Opus 69 — PASS** (every criterion ≥ 8, one point of margin, numbers discipline 9; 0 critical, 0 high, 1 medium, 7 low, all applied in §9e row 13); **Codex Round 9: 67 — NOT_YET** (one point short; numbers 7, every other criterion ≥ 8; its list is applied in §9e row 14). **Round 10: Opus 69 — PASS** (one point of margin again; 0 critical, 0 high, 1 medium, 6 low, all applied in §9e row 15); **Codex Round 10: 68 — PASS** (every criterion ≥ 8; 0 critical, 0 high, 2 medium, 8 low, all applied in §9e row 16). **Both reviewers pass the same version**; the residual lists are applied and checker-verified, and further slack is evidence work (§11), not design. **Round 12 (the v3 rebuild, both rubrics): Codex 68/80 + 34/40 — PASS; Opus 71/80 + 33/40 — NOT_YET** (§9h). **Round 13 (the same version for both): Codex 71/80 + 35/40 — PASS; Opus 73/80 + 35/40 — PASS** — both reviewers pass both rubrics on one version, so the bar is met; their bounded residual lists are applied in this version (§9i). The defect lists are in [`debate/`](ux-elevation/debate/). Both reviewers state that any slack above 68 requires evidence the document cannot supply: a 10-driver comprehension test on the copy ("aprox.", "gris", "por viaje", "en turno"), a 1-second chip test on a mount, and a measured install→"preparado" time on real phones. The "v2" column is the target for the re-score of this version, not a measured result.

All six named the chip's tray as C1's weakest element (some would turn the limits into a gauge, others drop them); the comparative lineups show a gauge is what makes a chip read as the template, so the tray stays and R14 measures whether drivers read it. **The walkthrough, run 4** (§11 R16, on the Round 19 kit, before 19b; per-task table in §9o): mean ease **≈5.4/7** (5.46 with all six judges, 5.42 without Codex's provisional walk; run 3: 5.60), ease against Uber **6.5/10** (6.7), would understand every screen **5.7/10** (5.8); 37 of 48 tasks succeeded and 11 were partial. Changing tu mínimo rose 5.3 → 5.5 and the first day held at 4.7; every offer fell 5.7 → 4.8 (five of six judges read D1's two "$…/km" rows as broken, fixed in 19b), a stopped overlay 5.2 → 5.0 and last week 6.0 → 5.8. **The gate is missed on every count:** ≈5.4 < 6.0, 6.5 < 7.5, three tasks under 5.5 (every offer 4.8, a stopped overlay 5.0, the first day 4.7), and C2's amarillo — a position, not a decision — named by four of six judges in both run 3 and run 4, a repeat the gate does not allow.

**Five runs: a plateau, then the prototype** (§11 R16; `blind-test/walkthrough/`):

| Run | Kit | Mean ease (1–7) | Ease against Uber (1–10) | Would understand every screen (1–10) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Round 15c | 5.48 | 6.5 | 5.7 |
| 2 | Round 17 | 5.50 | 6.5 | 6.0 |
| 3 | Round 18 | 5.60 | 6.7 | 5.8 |
| 4 | Round 19 | ≈5.4 (5.46) | 6.5 | 5.7 |
| 5 | Round 20c, the clickable prototype | 5.83 | 7.0 | 7.0 |

Three rounds of static fixes since run 1 (Rounds 17, 18 and 19) moved mean ease by less than 0.2. Each round lifted the tasks it targeted (changing tu mínimo 5.0 → 5.3 → 5.5 over runs 2–4), and another task fell on what the redraw newly showed (every offer in run 4). **Opus's call after run 4: further static frames will not close the gate. The next evidence is a clickable first run with real drivers (§11 R16's "later, real drivers") and the mounted one-second glance test (§11 R14), which is still open.** Round 19b (§9o) applies both Round 19 lists. **Run 5** (`blind-test/walkthrough/results-r20c-run5.md`), the first on the clickable prototype, stepped off the plateau — 5.83, every answer screen reached — but changing tu mínimo and the first day at 5.00 kept the gate shut; Round 21 targets both (§9r). The judges are models, the Android step was imagined and the Uber scores are self-estimated: not a rubric re-score and no real driver.

**Round 19b — the reviewers** ([`281`](ux-elevation/debate/281-round19b-codex-astra.md), [`282`](ux-elevation/debate/282-round19b-opus-5-5.md)): **Codex PASS — 71/80 + 34/40** (9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 8 · 9; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9; A3b's hard gate cleared, E1c and D1 resolved, numbers +1) and **Opus PASS — 75/80 + 34/40** (10 · 10 · **9** · 9 · 9 · 10 · 9 · 9, navigation −1 for B1's door, named by four of six run-4 judges; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9; no hard fail on either rubric). Both keep "comparable in use" at NOT_YET and both put the next evidence in the driver test plan, after repairing what would contaminate it — the prototype's jump from 14:40 / $548 to 21:10 / $1,026, B3's and B4's false "funcionando", every offer row opening the 14:32 detail, limit rings that neither stepped nor saved — plus Opus's C6 insets, B2's two outage starts, G4c's doubled cause and B1's door. The pre-test pass applies them (§9p) and has not been re-scored.

**Round 20 — the reviewers** ([`291`](ux-elevation/debate/291-round20-codex-astra.md), [`292`](ux-elevation/debate/292-round20-opus-5-5.md)), on the pre-test kit and its fixes: **Codex PASS — 71/80 + 34/40** (9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 8 · 9; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9; unchanged) and **Opus PASS — 76/80 + 34/40** (10 · 10 · **10** · 9 · 9 · 10 · 9 · 9, navigation +1 now that every Hoy door but the off one opens Semáforo; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9); no hard fail on either rubric. Both found the kit and prototype **not ready as written** for the same three blockers — the answers visible beside the phone, the 21:10 month under a 14:40 clock, the plan's disagreeing decision rules — and kept "comparable in use" at NOT_YET (Opus: feel yes, use still unmeasured; Codex: the repairs improve the instrument but supply no human evidence). The fixes are in §9q and have not been re-scored; the next evidence is run 5 with real drivers and the mounted glance test (§11 R14, R16).

**Round 20b — the reviewers** ([`294`](ux-elevation/debate/294-round20b-codex-astra.md), [`295`](ux-elevation/debate/295-round20b-opus-5-5.md)), confirming §9q: **Codex PASS — 71/80 + 34/40** and **Opus PASS — 76/80 + 34/40**, both unchanged, no hard fail on either rubric. Codex: every Round 20 finding resolved, no-go only for three plan items (task 8 first, the fifteen-driver comprehension gates, the real Kompara hand-off). Opus: N1–N10 resolved; go on a laptop, phone runs held until Android's back gesture and a reload stopped dropping the driver onto the 21:10 day. Both applied in Round 20c (§9q), not re-scored.

**Round 24 — the final verdicts** ([`321`](ux-elevation/debate/321-final-codex-astra.md), [`322`](ux-elevation/debate/322-final-opus-5-5.md)), on the Round 24b kit and prototype: **Codex PASS — 71/80 + 34/40** (9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 9 · 8 · 9; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9) and **Opus PASS — 76/80 + 34/40** (10 · 10 · 10 · 9 · 9 · 10 · 9 · 9; craft 8 · 8 · 9 · 9), both unchanged from Round 20b, no hard fail; the one-second glance untested on both. Codex: not ready as written, for one instrument correction (task 9's finished-versus-under-way trip). Opus: ready, after two plan-text edits (the neutral D2 probe; task 3's success line). Both applied in Round 24c (§9v), not re-scored.

## 11. Driver tests before building (residual disagreements)

| # | Question | v2 default | Evidence that settles it |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Position (the ▲ ◆ ▼ cue, the position phrase and the marker on the two limits) vs a verdict word | position + phrase + cue | 1-second exposures at 60 cm, 10–12 CDMX drivers incl. ≥ 3 with colour-vision deficiency, day and night; accuracy of take/pass vs their stated limits; false-"bad" rate on grey. |
| R2 | Typical default costs vs no net verdict until costs are entered | typical, labelled | Beta A/B: share of first real offers with a coloured chip; share who later edit costs; verdict flip rate when real costs replace typical (< 5 % ⇒ safe). |
| R3 | Second rate ($/h) on the chip | off since Round 16 (in Detalle de oferta) | Same 1-second test, one rate vs two: decision accuracy and time to decision; in ride-alongs, how often drivers open the detail for $/h. |
| R4 | Before-shift greeting | off | Beta A/B: time to discover a dead reader; opt-out rate (kill if > 25 %). |
| R5 | Trial auto-start at week close for account holders | explicit | A/B on day-30 conversion and "¿me van a cobrar?" support contacts. |
| R6 | Tus apps free vs Premium | free inside the window | Fake-door test: trial starts vs Comparar visits and week-2 retention. |
| R7 | IMSS income basis | ship after legal confirms | Legal B-038: which income the reform counts; if unclear, show "Te pagó la app" without a threshold line. |
| R8 | Recompute history on cost change | effective date; offer for km/L | How often drivers correct km/L in week 1; 5 interviews on whether a changed past total feels like a bug. |
| R9 | Three tabs vs four | three | Tree test + first-click: "¿Dónde ves si ganas más que otros de tu ciudad?", "¿Dónde ves lo del IMSS?"; switch only if findability < 70 %. |
| R10 | Bringing back any overlay editor | removed | Only if drivers in ride-alongs demonstrably need to change their limits between offers and a parked-only trigger exists (e.g. the host app idle for ≥ 30 s); otherwise Tu semáforo is enough. |
| R11 | Cost question before or after the permission | after — on Hoy, the next morning (B4 → A5; Round 18) | Beta A/B on "reader preparado at 24 h" and on the share of drivers who later edit costs. |
| R12 | IMSS bars at all before the legal basis is confirmed | none until B-038 | Legal memo; then a provenance audit against official records for real cases. |
| R13 | Per-app hours from the reader (if a reliable foreground-time split can be measured) | not used | Device study: compare reader-split hours with statement hours on real weeks; enable per-app "por hora" only if the error is small and stable. |
| R14 | **Mounted glance test of la regla v16** (its four states against the blind test's best generic chip — the split verdict tile, a coloured block with a road-sign icon and a verdict word, the glance leader of lineups 7 and 8) | la regla v16 | 12 CDMX drivers, ≥ 3 colour-vision deficient; phone mounted at 60 cm while the driver fixates a road video; 1 s exposures at ~25° eccentricity, day and night, **on two host geometries** (Opus R15): C1's (the card at 64 % of the screen height, the full plate in the foot slot) and C12's Uber-proportioned card (at 40 %, the puck centred above it, the compact chip) — on Uber the second is the common case, and every blind lineup used the first. **The residual risk it settles (§9j):** model judges put v16 in the glance lead group (3.5 of 11 against 3.2–3.3), but their glance ranks are predictions that moved with the framing (lineup 7), so the test pits **the position phrase, shape and marker against a verdict word with road-sign icons**, on first exposure and after A1. **Pass:** state identification ≥ 95 % and no worse than the verdict-word chip, with **zero gris-as-rojo** calls and the C9 plate not grouped with the host's dark banner; after A1, ≥ 8 of 10 answer "¿Qué quiere decir el amarillo?" correctly (since Round 23 an answer with "cumple" or "entre" counts; the probe also records whether "Cumple tu mínimo", a verb, is read as an order). It also measures how often drivers take the host's fare or gross $/km for the chip's net rate (Opus R14-08), whether the limits' 2dp lines and 15sp labels carry at 60 cm (R14-09), **the marker's size** (the 22 × 5dp bar in the state's tint since Round 17, against a larger one, Opus R15 §3: does a larger marker buy back signature without costing the glance), **the C9 manoeuvre case** (the plate placed clear of the turn the banner announces, against the foot slot over it: do drivers miss or misjudge the turn), whether the compact chip (no phrase line) is read as well as the full plate, and whether the last-resort placement (the verdict over a map pin) costs anything in motion. If the verdict word wins, the choice between it and position goes back to the founder before the overlay is built. ~1 week; a gate for the Android overlay build (§12), outside design scope (founder fork 5). |
| R15 | **Greyscale render test** | the cue, phrase and marker carry the state without hue | Render C1–C5, C7 and C12's compact chip in greyscale on the same host, at the same position, day and night: the four states must be four distinct patterns (▲ with the marker above the lines · ◆ with the marker between them · ▼ with the marker below them · a neutral band with no cue, no marker and dashed lines), the cue legible in each, and gris must carry no cue and no marker. Since Round 23 the same render includes Hoy's status glyphs (the dot, the hollow ring, the slashed ring, the pause bars, the ring with "!"), which must stay distinct without hue (j3, run 7; Opus `312` §4: the words carry each state, so the glyphs change only if this render fails). Runs on every chip change before R14, and as a render diff in CI once the overlay exists. |
| R16 | **Usability walkthrough (repeatable)** | Run 1 (Round 15c kit): mean ease 5.5/7, 42 of 48 tasks succeeded, ease against Uber 6.5/10 (§9l); run 2 (Round 17 kit): mean ease 5.5/7, 40 of 48, ease against Uber 6.5/10, understand every screen 6.0/10 (§9m); run 3 (Round 18 kit): mean ease 5.6/7, 40 of 48, ease against Uber 6.7/10, understand every screen 5.8/10 (§9n, §10); run 4 (Round 19 kit): mean ease ≈5.4/7 (5.46), 37 of 48, ease against Uber 6.5/10, understand every screen 5.7/10 (§9o, §10) — four runs on a plateau (5.48 · 5.50 · 5.60 · ≈5.4); run 5 (Round 20c kit, the first on the clickable prototype; six persona judges through a participant harness, `blind-test/walkthrough/results-r20c-run5.md`): mean ease 5.83/7, 54 of 54 answer screens reached (53 yes, 1 partial), ease against Uber 7.0/10, understand every screen 7.0/10 — off the plateau, not through the gate (changing tu mínimo and the first day at 5.00; §9r); run 6 (Round 21b kit, `results-r21b-run6.md`): 5.93/7, 52 of 54, ease against Uber 7.0/10, understand 7.2/10 (§9s); run 7 (Round 22 kit, `results-r22-run7.md`): 5.94/7, 52 of 54, ease against Uber 7.3/10, understand 7.3/10, the trip's money against the day's in the top three of four judges in both runs 6 and 7 (§9t); run 8 (Round 23b kit, the corrected instrument, `results-r23b-run8.md`): 6.17/7, 54 of 54, ease against Uber 7.5/10, understand 7.5/10, lowest task 5.67 — the walkthrough gate met — and Opus's readiness criteria five of six, the trip's basis against the day's again in the top three of four judges (j2, j3, j5, j6; §9u) | Run on every version that changes navigation, names or money words, before a rubric re-score. Six context-free judges (models without project context; later, real drivers) each play a first-time CDMX driver on the contact sheets and the screen-id list, with two Uber Driver screenshots, and do the same eight tasks: (1) from B1, what have I kept today and is it reading; (2) at C2, in one second, is the offer worth it and why (since Round 23: what the offer says at a glance, then whether they would take it and why); (3) from Hoy, every offer today with its $/km (since Round 23: what each leaves per km); (4) from Hoy, last week after all costs, car included; (5) from Hoy, which app paid more per km last week; (6) change tu mínimo to $6.50/km; (7) the overlay stopped during the shift — why, and what to do (since Round 23: the semáforo missing from the offers early in the shift); (8) first day, from A1 to a working overlay. Per task: the path as screen ids with the element tapped, taps, success (yes / partial / no), where they hesitated, ease on the Single Ease Question (1–7); then the three biggest problems with a fix, ease against Uber (1–10) and "would I understand every screen" (1–10), tallied per task. **Pass for a version — the walkthrough gate (Round 18; Opus `252`):** **mean ease ≥ 6.0/7, ease against Uber ≥ 7.5/10 and no task below 5.5 mean ease**, with no "no" and no defect named by ≥ 4 of 6 judges that the previous run also named; a finding named by every judge (Round 17: the two meanings of "Te quedan") blocks the version until fixed. Run 2 met none of the three numbers (5.5 · 6.5 · the first day at 4.2, changing tu mínimo at 5.0, a stopped overlay at 5.2), nor did run 3 (5.6 · 6.7 · the first day at 4.7, a stopped overlay at 5.2, changing tu mínimo at 5.3), nor run 4 on the Round 19 kit (≈5.4 · 6.5 · every offer at 4.8, a stopped overlay at 5.0, the first day at 4.7), which also repeated a defect named by four of six judges in run 3 (C2's amarillo as a position, not a decision), nor run 5 on the prototype (5.83 · 7.0 · changing tu mínimo and the first day at 5.00), whose defects named by every judge — no confirmation after Guardar, two «Abrir ajustes» and a dead end, «control total» — Round 21 removes. **After four runs on a plateau, Opus's call: further static frames will not close the gate; the next evidence is a clickable first run with real drivers and the mounted glance test (R14).** **The real-driver test comes next** ([`driver-test-plan.md`](ux-elevation/driver-test-plan.md), on `prototype.html` after Round 21, §9q, §9r): 10 CDMX drivers (5 renters, 5 owners) on nine tasks — the eight above, task 3 now succeeding on D1a scrolled to 08:15 with its rates read (whether the driver says «sin el coche» unprompted is recorded apart, Round 24c), plus task 9, «Desde Hoy, busca cuánto te dejó tu último viaje terminado.» (B1 → Ver todas las ofertas de hoy → the 11:20 row → D2e, $133 sin el coche; at 14:40 the 14:32 row reads «En curso», Rounds 24c–24d); the prototype in participant mode (no answers and no frame ids on the driver's screen; the facilitator reads each task aloud from the plan and follows a second window, `?facilitador=1`, with the answers and the live log); an Uber baseline on three matched tasks (task 1 ↔ today's earnings, 4 ↔ last week's, 9 ↔ one trip's detail; half the drivers do the Uber tasks first); task 8 split across two devices (A1 → A2 → A3 in the prototype, then, at A3's «Abrir ajustes», a real Android phone for Settings instead of the stand-in A3s, timed to the return, one ease rating after it; Round 21); the first tap of tasks 6 and 7 logged, which shows whether Hoy's door is found as the way to Semáforo (task 6) and how the driver reaches Kompara from C13 (task 7: the notification in the shade, H6b — a drag down from the top on a phone, a drag down from or a tap on the status bar in the prototype, never prompted — or the host bar's "Abrir Kompara", as quiet as the other host controls; the pre-test fixes moved its start from C6); and one pre-registered rule that passes only when all five hold — mean ease ≥ 6.0 over the nine tasks (5.6–5.99 adds five drivers; < 5.6 fails), Kompara within 0.5 of the same drivers' Uber mean on the matched tasks or above it, no task under 5.5 that is also a top-named problem (from the closing «¿Qué fue lo más difícil?»), the comprehension probes (R17; since Round 23 also an offer detail's trip and day, on D2e alone since Round 24d), and no reported money inconsistency; the 1–10 rating against Uber is reported, not gated. **Round 23 — the stop rule (both reviewers):** one model run 8 on the corrected instrument (personas who drive Uber and DiDi with Lo del coche entered, the framing sentence after task 8, no invented errors, task 2 screenshot-only, tasks 3 and 7 re-worded; §9t), then no more model walkthroughs, whatever its mean; for model runs Opus retires the gate above in favour of six readiness criteria (54/54 answer screens with misses ≤ 0.2 per task; no task under 5.5; no defect in the top three of ≥ 4 judges in both run 7 and run 8; no money contradiction true of the fixture; Kompara at or above the judges' own Uber estimate on tasks 1, 4 and 9; B1's $548 read as after the car by 6 of 6), the mean and the score against Uber reported, not gated; "comparable in use to Uber Driver" is claimed only from real drivers under the plan's rule, plus R14 for the chip. |
| R17 | **The money definition with renters and owners** | "Llevas" (an open period; "Te quedaron" a closed one, Round 21) after the car by calendar day; a signed ink figure, never rojo, from the first trip | 10 CDMX drivers — 5 who rent (pay a weekly cuenta) and 5 who own (with a crédito or seguro entered as Lo del coche) — read B1 at **$548** ("Llevas hoy · aprox.") and B8 at **−$100** (the 14:32 trip alone; Round 18 draws it as an alternate frame), then E1c's past day at −$214 ("Sin viajes; lo del coche corre igual"). Ask: "¿Qué quiere decir este número?" and "¿Por qué es negativo?". **Pass:** ≥ 8 of 10 read $548 as "what I keep after my costs, the car included" and match it to E8's week; none reads the minus as an error, a debt to Kompara or money to earn ("te faltan"), and none takes the ink figure for a verdict. Also measure whether owners without Lo del coche miss the car clause, and whether drivers expect the chip's rate to include the car (it must not). If renters and owners split, the choice of definition goes back to the founder before the ledger is built. **D2 probe (Round 23, Opus `312` §2; neutral since Round 24c, Opus 322 defect 1: asked in the row's own words it tested string matching, not the basis; on D2e alone since Round 24d, because over Hoy at 14:40 D2's trip is still under way and a direct D2 shows the 21:10 day's $1,026):** open D2e (D1a → the 11:20 row) and ask three questions — «¿Cuánto te dejó este viaje?» → $133; «Esos $133, ¿ya tienen descontado lo que pagas del coche?» → no; «¿Y en todo el día, cuánto llevas ya descontando el coche?» → $548; **pass:** ≥ 8 of 10 on each; the second carries rule 4. If 3 or more of the 5 renters still ask for the trip's figure after the car, the per-trip question goes to the founder with that evidence (a per-trip share of the car was rejected in §9t). **Recorded, not gated** (Opus `322` item 5; Round 24e, §9x): which name drivers use for the car's cost, «lo del coche» or «el coche», and whether anyone takes the two for different costs. |

## 12. Engineering implications (gates, not design)
- **One executable fixture:** `design-system/ui_kits/android-app-v2/fixture.json` holds the raw inputs (costs, floors, car cost, shifts, weeks by app, statement, cohort deciles, IMSS factor) and `check-fixture.py` derives the displayed figures and asserts each one INSIDE its named frame (screen text only), plus strings in this spec, the free-window date rule, the bar charts, caption lints, stale patterns, the old vocabulary and the state phrase of every chip's accessible name and every offer row; its last line prints the exact counts (263 figures in 80 frames, 20 chips and 86 offer rows at the time of writing, after Round 21, which added the inline limits editor — G2d's helper and Guardar, G2e, G4i — A3s and A3n, "Llevas" on every open period, the not-taken trip label, "vie $1,166", B2's text button and ten Round 21 stale patterns; Round 20 had added the 14:40 month — E3, E3d, D3b, E4, E5 — with its spec strings, G4h and G1b, D1a's and D1b's ten rows, the per-frame bans "21:10" on E3, E3d and D3b, "Redondeado" on E3d and D3b and "Ofertas · 23" on D1, and the old month figures as stale patterns; the pre-test fixes had added C13, D1b, G2d, G4f and G4g and per-frame forbidden strings — "permiso" on C6, "Semáforo apagado" on C13, "ayer" on G4e; the pre-test had added D1a's, D3a's, D2b's and D2c's figures and a stale pattern for "según el lector," in any frame). It does not measure rendered geometry: placement numbers (card tops, feet, marker clearances) are measured from the 2× renders, and a render-measured placement check is owed. It does not compare spec prose with frame prose, and captions are linted only for known-stale phrases. It runs after every mockup or spec edit and in CI; a figure that is not derivable from the fixture may not appear on a screen.
- **Tokens:** the verdict green is **#187a36** (white 13px text reaches 5.4:1; the previous #1b8a3a gave 4.43:1); amarillo keeps dark text (#1a1300 on #f2b705); rojo unchanged. The same value goes into `design-system/tokens` before the chip is built. **v3:** the product palette is the paper/ink set of §4 (`--paper` — white `#FFFFFF` by day since Round 14 — `--paper-2`, `--ink`/`-2`/`-3`, `--rule`, the verdict triple, `--chip-ink`, `--k` and the marker tints `--mark-verde` / `--mark-amarillo` / `--mark-rojo`, Round 18), implemented in `base-v3.css` and to be mirrored in `design-system/tokens` and `theme/Color.kt`; the slate and emerald scales, `chipNeutral`, info-blue, amber and the second red are deleted, not deprecated.
- **Chip geometry (la regla v16):** one `Regla` component (`.regla`; exact markup per state in `_regla-markup.html`): a 240 × 112 ink plate, radius 14; a fixed 56dp band on top in every state — the solid 30dp ▲ ◆ ▼, the deciding rate at 30sp with "/km neto" and the position phrase — over the ink tray: two **categorical** labelled lines, tu meta upper and tu mínimo lower (15sp labels, 2dp lines), never proportional to the money, and a 22 × 5dp marker bar in the state's tint (`--mark-*`) above, between or below them; no $/h on the plate (it lives in the offer detail); gris = a neutral band, no cue, no marker, dashed lines; the band's accessible name starts with the position phrase. The position comes from a **per-host validated safe band** (the bottom of the host's top chrome to the top of its offer card, measured after layout): the plate's bottom edge sits 16dp above the card (top = card top − plate height − 16, from the card's measured top: 417 on the standard host, whose card starts at 545; 307.4 at 1.3×, C10), 12dp from the right edge; the map-marker exclusions (pickup pin, destination, the driver's puck and, during navigation, the next manoeuvre, each with 16dp to its outer bounds, a pin's stroke included) pick the first clear slot in §6's order; when no slot holds the full plate the phrase line drops first — the compact chip, `.regla.compact`, 240 × 80 with both limits (C12) — and only when the band cannot clear them either does the foot slot win — the verdict beats a pin (the mounted test, §11 R14, settles whether that costs anything in motion); stored per host app and orientation and revalidated on every layout change; **suppression** only when that band is shorter than even the compact chip + 32dp (112dp at 1×; the full plate needs 144) (the offer is still logged in Tu día with the reason "sin semáforo en pantalla" and counted on the Semáforo screen, "No cupo el semáforo", whose cause reads "no cupo en la pantalla"); one geometry for every chip (the overlay, the A1/B3/G2 previews and the night host; frames add only a position); **every size scales with the system font** and the plate grows with it (1.3× → 312 × 146, C10) — `VerdictChipUi`'s 232dp step and the K are retired; state changes are atomic swaps; C6 is a separate 240 × 72dp non-interactive ink tab (three lines: "Semáforo apagado", its cause, where to fix it; its foot on the regla's, 457–529) with no lines and no band. Verified by the greyscale render (R15) before, and the mounted glance test (R14) after, the overlay is built.
- **Fonts:** bundle Barlow 400/600 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500/600/700 as app assets; a build-time check that the shipped files carry `tnum` and `lnum` and a device check of 13sp Barlow at 1×; fallback Roboto (UI) and Archivo SemiCondensed 600 (money); never horizontal scaling; no weight 800/900; Inter removed.
- **Theme:** follow the system (`isSystemInDarkTheme()`) with a persisted Ajustes override "Tema · Automático / Claro / Oscuro"; no Material You dynamic colour; the overlay ignores the theme — chip ink and the verdict triple are fixed in both modes.
- **The mark:** one `Mark` composable (16 × 24dp, 18 × 28 in `OfferRow`; states v/a/r/g) is the only leading glyph in offer rows (Hoy, Tu día) and never appears in Comparar; no icon tiles, circled glyphs or coloured dots anywhere in rows; `VerdictBadge` is removed.

- **Golden fixture + CI:** one fixture (costs, floors, a week of offers/shifts, lo del coche, a cohort with its basis) drives every mock, preview, demo and screenshot; a check fails when a displayed number differs from engine output or when two screens show the same period in different scopes without a label.
- **Engine:** missing fare ⇒ gris, not red; no zero-cost fallback when marginal cost is unavailable; no automatic unit substitution; unknown is a state (`NetProfitEngine`, `VerdictChipState`); offer lifecycle (offered / accepted-estimated / confirmed / expired / unknown) decides what enters earnings.
- **Readiness model:** the semáforo's *preparado* (service bound) vs *funcionando* (a real host offer parsed since the last bind, today) with decay, each state drawn with its status glyph (§4); per-app "lectura comprobada"; an explicit *en pausa* state with auto-resume and alert suppression; persist "última lectura" per app; the supported-app list comes from the parser registry.
- **Capture lanes:** one disclosure covering both; consent prompts only where the device actually needs them (API < 30 or a disabled silent lane) — recovery follows capability, not API level alone.
- **Costs:** typical defaults labelled and surfaced in the hero's quiet line; effective-dated cost changes; the weekly fixed cost ("lo del coche") allocated by calendar day and applied to every "te quedan" total — day, week and month, today included — and never to the chip, the verdict, "te deja" or a rate (Round 17; replace the unread `dailyFixedCosts`); a day before its first trip shows no figure, and a negative result is signed, never clamped (§4).
- **Trial:** a server-granted promotional entitlement separate from Play subscriptions; explicit start; no automatic conversion. Validate against Play's payments policy.
- **Verification:** eligibility checked before the sales action; cached positive entitlement survives fetch failures; "Importa una semana para desbloquear" removed as a post-purchase state.
- **Import:** consent step on every route including `ShareImportActivity`; one parse; reconciliation by app/period/field instead of replacement; retention wording tied to the backend's actual behaviour.
- **IMSS:** the reference amount served from remote config with year and source; rules and income basis validated (B-038) before any bar or threshold line; withholding estimates and the word "retenciones" withheld until validated; the official verification link is the primary action.
- **Notifications:** channels "Estado del semáforo" and "Resúmenes"; one deduplicated incident notification naming the cause, suppressed while paused, whose tap opens Hoy's off state (B2) — with the permission lost it is the only signal during the shift, its small icon waiting in the status bar (C13); no amounts in any notification; summaries deferred while a ride app is foreground; `setPublicVersion` with titles only.
- **Overlay:** drag only (inert tap and long-press); per-host persisted position with revalidation; suppression when no safe position fits; the "Semáforo apagado" tab (C6) is non-interactive and time-bounded, and only for a stopped capture with the service alive ("Dejamos de leer tus ofertas"); a lost permission draws nothing over the host (C13), so its recovery lives in the notification and Hoy; no interactive surface over the host without an offer.
- **Data flows and consent:** the contribution opt-in (default off) gates any upload of weekly aggregates to the benchmark population; the anonymous parse-success telemetry is either disclosed (as now) or removed; account deletion cancels pending uploads, revokes the opt-in, and has an external request route (kompara.mx/borrar); the Data-safety form lists derived totals as transmitted when an account exists.
- **Period accounting:** every total is built from dated records with an outcome (offered / taken-estimated / confirmed / cancelled / expired / unknown) and a cost version; income and running costs sum by date; the recurring car cost is allocated by calendar day to every period, today and the open week included, so a day's figure is the same on Hoy, Tu día, Semana and Mes and a week is the sum of its days (a CI check: Σ of a week's day results equals the week hero within the stated rounding peso); an undated import is never distributed across months — wholly-contained weeks count toward monthly payment totals, boundary-crossing ones stay unallocated — and never supplies a net without distance; imports reconcile by app, period and field with matching coverage for numerator and denominator; cancellation of an individual trip requires itemized evidence (the driver's tap on the row, or an itemized statement) — aggregate statements reconcile totals only.
- **Cleanup:** 43 + 12 dead strings, `HomeUiText.kt`, `HomeViewModel.kt`, `overlay/VerdictColors.kt`, the share-card variant toggle; move hardcoded copy (recommendations, Comparar headers, share caption, OCR notifications, city names) into `strings.xml` with accents.
- **Design system:** add the v3 tokens, `Regla` (the chip) and `ReaderStoppedTab`, `Mark`, `OfferRow`, `Observation`, `ListRow`, `StepperField`, `LimitControl`, `GreyButton`, `MaskedValue`, `HeroQuietLine`, `TextTabs`, `StatusLine` (with the Round 17 status glyphs and Hoy's door row, Round 18: into Semáforo in its state, the limits its first row — Round 19's door to the limits reverted in the pre-test), `FigureRow`, `BestBar`, `CompareBars`, `StateLegend` and the drawn icon set (`_head.html`'s sprite → vector drawables) to `design-system/` and the Compose components; remove `Card`-as-surface, `VerdictBadge`, `StatusPill`, `ScopeLine`, `LaneEditor`, `ShiftLine`, the dashed `CarLine`, the Material icon set and the segmented control; a CI check that `:overlay` defines no colours of its own and that no screen uses a token outside the v3 set.

## Appendix — mockup index

The kit [`design-system/ui_kits/android-app-v2/`](../design-system/ui_kits/android-app-v2/) assembles `index.html` from `_head.html` + `part-a…h.html` + `part-n.html` on `base-v3.css` (its last blocks: the Round 13/14 system layer, the Round 14 consolidation, the Round 15 components, the Round 16 chip, the Round 15b residuals and the Round 17 block — status glyphs, offer-row type, Ganancias' figure row, the marker bar — then the Round 18 marker tints and the Round 21 blocks — the worded period steps (`.period.steps`, `.spark i.best span.dia`) and the limits' filled field (`.limit .lf`, its focus, helper and cue); the drawn icon sprite, with the Round 18 gear, is in `_head.html`; edit the parts, never `index.html`); **116 frames** = the 106 screens below + the night set N1–N10 (Round 13 added C8–C11, N9 and N10; Round 15b added C12; Round 18 added B7, B8 and E1c; Round 19 added A3b, B9, E1d, G2b, G4b and G4c; Round 19b added G2c and G4d; the pre-test added D1a, D2b, D2c, D3a and G4e; the pre-test fixes added C13, D1b, G2d, G4f and G4g; their second batch added D2d and H6b; Round 20 added G1b and G4h; Round 21 added A3s, A3n, G2e and G4i and merged A3b into A3; Round 21b added A4b; Round 22 added E1e; Round 23 added B2r, E1f and A4r; Round 24c added B3n and D2e). [`android-app-v2-r10/`](../design-system/ui_kits/android-app-v2-r10/) is the frozen Round 10 reference (`base.css`, 66 frames) that every frame outside 120 §4's merged wireframes keeps its content and states from. `check-fixture.py` runs after every edit; `render-frames.py` and `sheets.py` produce the PNGs the reviewers score.
